So the assistant cannot move a job to `Shipped/ invoiced`, `MTX FAT`, `Crated`, `Ready for Packaging` or any of the other twelve, and because the enum is also what it READS as the vocabulary, it does not know they are options — asked to mark something shipped it will either refuse or pick the nearest of five, which is a different thing than the user asked for. **SAME SHAPE AS #447 AND #442, FROM A THIRD CLIENT:** a consumer narrows a user-editable vocabulary to a fixed set it was written against, and the narrowing is invisible from the outside. The difference, and it is the reason this is a smaller entry: the AI schema CONSTRAINS A WRITE rather than destroying a read. A tool call outside the enum is rejected by the model's own validation, so the AI cannot flatten a status it does not recognise the way iOS's decode does — it simply cannot reach it. Nothing is lost; something is merely unavailable. THE FIX is to build the enum from `orgSettings.statusOpts` at the point the tool schema is assembled, which is already per-session data the component holds — the schema is constructed in the browser with the org loaded, so there is no new fetch. WORTH CHECKING AT THE SAME TIME, not measured here: whether `pri` has the identical problem (`priOpts` is editable the same way and the schema pins `["Low","Medium","High"]`), and whether any other enum in that schema is a frozen copy of a list the org can edit.

449. [FIXED AND LIVE 2026-10-07 22:53Z — 4bfffff, pushed on its own] **EVERY WEB SAVE AFTER THE FIRST IN A SESSION IS DISCARDED, AND REPORTED AS SAVED.** #339 (42d81fb) made `doSave` send a delta envelope `{ upsert, delete }` once a write has been acknowledged — an OBJECT. `saveTasks` (`src/api.js:54`) still opens with the 2026-04 guard `if (!Array.isArray(tasks)) { console.warn("saveTasks blocked — not an array"); return { ok: true }; }`, so the envelope never leaves the browser and the caller is told it succeeded. The first save of a page load has no ack, goes as a full array and lands; every save after it is dropped, and the 30s poll then REPLACES the client's tree with the server's (`[poll] state REPLACED`), so the edit vanishes from the screen too — a job created in that window disappears entirely. **REPRODUCED** in a sandbox (real web app + real `tasks.js` on an in-memory store, synthetic org): three add-task saves in one session, round 1 stored, rounds 2–3 logged `delta: 1 upsert` and `saveTasks blocked — not an array Object`, no POST reached the server. **LIVE SINCE THE 2026-10-07 18:04Z DEPLOY (0235304).** Matrix's `tasks.json` took bursts of writes seconds apart before it and exactly two after (18:57:52 one drag; 22:11:13 no content change). Nothing in S3 was destroyed by this — the stored copy is what existed before each lost edit — and what was lost was never written, so S3 versions cannot recover it. iOS (bare `[Job]`) is unaffected. Why the suite missed it: `delta-write-test.mjs` exercises `deltaWrite.js` and the server, never `saveTasks`, the one hop between them. This is also #3 of Trey's 2026-10-07 report: dates and an assignee need two saves (the grid refuses an assignee until dates exist), and the second is the one discarded.

450. [FIXED 2026-10-07 — CORRECTED IN PLACE, R2: first logged against the wrong component] **THE JOBS GRID'S DATE CELLS OPENED A CALENDAR NOBODY COULD SEE.** Clicking START, END or DUE on a grid row mounts `TraqsDatePicker autoOpen` inside a row with `overflow: hidden`, and the picker drew its calendar in place, so the row clipped it. Measured in the sandbox: the calendar was in the DOM and `elementFromPoint` at its month header returned the next row's text. The cell reads "Select date", a click on it toggles the invisible calendar shut, and nothing is written — Trey's "blank, unclickable field", and the reason a task's dates could not be set from the grid. **THIS ENTRY FIRST NAMED THE WRONG SITE:** `DateField` at `TRAQS.jsx:14243` (and `:13596`). That is the CUSTOM-COLUMN date cell; START/END/DUE are `TraqsDatePicker` at `:13862`, `:13872`, `:13880`. A fix applied to `:14243` changed nothing in the sandbox, which is how it was caught; it was reverted, and `:14243`/`:13596` remain UNMEASURED (same row container, plausibly the same clip). **FIX:** the three grid pickers use the picker's existing `portal` mode (calendar on document.body, `position: fixed`, outside-click already treats it as inside). Measured after: calendar VISIBLE, START and END each save (as #449 delta saves), and with an assignee the task draws on the schedule. `scripts/grid-date-portal-test.mjs`, 10 assertions, wired; red on the unfixed code (3 failures), 8 mutants, 8 caught. Unchanged and not defects: the Edit Job page has no date input below the job (by design, `:31295`); task hours there are present and editable; the grid's HRS cell on a task row has no editor.

451. [FIXED 2026-10-08, COMPILED, UNSHIPPED — rides with the next Mac build alongside #447 and #457] **iOS STOPPED AT JOB LEVEL ON THE SCREENS WHERE TASKS MATTER.** Hierarchy is PARENT → JOB → TASK (`Job` → `Panel` → `Operation`). iOS has modelled all three since its first commit (ad16509, 2026-03-06: `struct Operation`, `Panel.subs: [Operation]`), and the original JobDetailView iterated ops — so this is a DEFECT in views that stop short, not unbuilt modelling. Where it stops: ALL JOBS (`AllJobsCard`, TasksView.swift:615, :846, :856) builds `TaskAssignment(op: nil)` for every panel and never walks `panel.subs`; YOUR TASKS (`myTasks`, :670–693) does reach ops, but only ones you are on, falling back to a panel card at :689; `JobDetailPopup` (the only detail view reachable from the list) renders ops behind `if appState.isBusinessTier` (:169) AND a collapsed disclosure (:366), read-only, no Start. Clock-in (TasksView.swift:1570 → AppState `completeJobClockIn` → POST timeclock `jobClockIn` with `opId`) sends `opId: task.op?.id`, so every ALL JOBS and panel-fallback card clocks in at JOB level with `opId` nil. The one piece of UNBUILT work: there has never been an iOS way to choose and clock into a task you are not already assigned to. Fix size, estimated: TasksView.swift (`panelsFor`, `panelsInWindow`, AllJobsCard op expansion), the AppState mirrors at :3441–3484, optionally JobDetailPopup.swift (Start on an op, default-expand). No model or API change — the payload already carries `opId`. **CONFIRMED BY SCREENSHOT 2026-10-08:** the ALL JOBS header read "0 of 3 tasks" and rendered three JOB cards — tasks were not on the phone at all. **FIXED (3 Swift files + 1 new + tests):** (1) TASKS RENDER UNDER THEIR JOB — `AllJobsCard` expands a parent to its jobs, each collapsed by default as "1 of 3 tasks", expanding to its tasks as the standard task blocks; the header counts tasks. (2) START CLOCKS THE TASK — every unit under a job with tasks is task-level (`op` set), so `beginJobClockIn` carries the `opId`; `TaskCardV1`'s Start is unchanged and was always correct for an op card. (3) A WORKER CAN REACH A TASK THEY ARE NOT ON — ALL JOBS now lists jobs you are assigned on too (it dropped any job you were on anywhere "so it never appears twice", which hid the OTHER tasks on your own job — the ones you cover); a colleague's task shows NOT ASSIGNED. The Active tab keeps "not yours", so its sections do not duplicate. ALSO: someone on a job's team but on none of its tasks got a job-level card whose Start clocked the job — 12 such memberships at Matrix, measured 2026-10-08; they now get the job's unfinished tasks (`TasksView.myTasks` and its mirror `AppState.myAssignments`, which feeds Home's today list, changed together). One rule in one place: `Services/TaskUnits.swift`; `TaskUnitsTests` (Swift Testing, 5 cases) compiled, not run. **START ON A JOB WITH NO TASKS — what it does and what it should do.** TODAY: every ALL JOBS card was job-level, so Start clocked into the JOB (`opId` nil) whether or not the job had tasks; the server accepts a job-only clock-in. SHOULD: a job with no tasks KEEPS a job-level Start, because the deepest node is the unit of work — the rule the schedule, `JobShifts` and `myAssignments` already share, and the shape a simple job is built in (`SimpleJob.swift`: one flat sub, no ops); hiding it would strand those jobs. A job WITH tasks offers Start only on its tasks. Measured at Matrix 2026-10-08: 7 of 123 jobs have no tasks, none of them current (all Finished or past), none with logged hours. **RULED 2026-10-08 (Trey): TIME IS LOGGED ONLY AT THE LOWEST LEVEL** — "you should only be able to clock into the tasks, or the lowest hierarchy of the job that is created." So a job with tasks never offers a way to log time; a job with no tasks is the lowest level and does. Enforced at both ends: `TaskUnits.isClockable` gates `TaskCardV1`'s Start (the app's only Start; Stop is separate, so a legacy job-level clock can still be stopped), and `AppState.beginJobClockIn` refuses a job-level clock-in on a job that has tasks. The count pills now count clockable units; the unused panel-level builder `panelsInWindow` is gone. **NOTED, NOT CHANGED:** the web's Start picker lists only tasks, so a job with no tasks has no web Start at all — the two clients disagree for the general/simple-job shape; and the Jobs list's count pills/heatmap (`allTasks`) still count other people's work per job, not per task.

452. [FIXED 2026-10-07] **THE WEB APP CRASHED AT PHONE WIDTH.** At 390px (CDP device emulation, sandbox) the app rendered the error boundary after load: `Rendered more hooks than during the previous render` at `renderMobileApp`. `renderMobileApp` declared `useState` for the More sheet (since 7dbbd6d, 2026-03-23) and is a plain function called as `{isMobile ? renderMobileApp() : …}`, so the hook ran on some renders and not others. **FIX:** `moreOpen` hoisted to the App's top level beside `isMobile`. Measured after: 390px loads with no error boundary, the More sheet opens, and desktop↔phone flips in one session do not crash. `scripts/render-hooks-test.mjs`, a ratchet over all 30 `renderX` helpers (each is called conditionally by construction, so a hook in any of them is the same crash) with must-catch/must-pass fixtures and an input guard; wired; red on the unfixed code (2 failures), 4 mutants: 3 caught by assertion, the gutted scanner exits 2. Why it surfaced only now was not investigated.

453. [ANSWERED AND RESTORED 2026-10-07] **ONE WRITE ON 2026-10-03 DELETED EVERY OVERDUE UNIT — 282 OPS, 5 PANELS, 1 JOB.** `tasks.json` version `OR0f4hkR…` (2026-10-03T20:55:07Z; 549 KB against 629 KB in `ZTSe_zIY…`, 20:39:18Z). **IT DELETED RECORDS; IT WAS NOT THE HISTORY-HIDING WORK,** which is render-only. The removed set is EXACTLY the Overdue tray's set: `overdueUnits` from `a00855d^:src/dragMove.js`, run on the pre-strip version with today = 2026-10-03, returns the same 282 ops + 5 panels + 1 job, zero difference either way, and nothing else in the write changed. (`402035 - Golden Chest` qualified only because its status is lowercase `finished` — #445.) **NO DEPLOYED CODE COULD HAVE DONE IT:** the live build was `cd24321`; its tray offered drag-to-reschedule only, and the schedule's Select All picks drawn bars, which history is not. The write lands 5 minutes before `a00855d` ("remove the Overdue tray") was committed, and Trey was running a dev server that day — **`netlify dev` writes to the PRODUCTION bucket** (the local `.env` carries the live S3 credentials, and the CLI also merges the site's own env), so a work-in-progress build that dropped the overdue units from state and autosaved produces exactly this. That is #454, and this is one instance of it. **RESTORED 2026-10-07 ~22:56Z** by targeted re-insert, not a version rollback (304 writes since; 12 of the 36 affected jobs edited again): each unit put back under its pre-strip parent in its pre-strip order, 402035 un-tombstoned, one If-Match write against ETag `371c6809…` of version `4mgfY7OM…` (that version is the rollback point). Dry run first: 288 restorations, 287 ids added, 0 lost, 0 other jobs changed, only additions. Watched afterwards across later writes from others (22:58Z, 00:29Z): 288 of 288 present at every check through 01:52Z.

454. [TRIPWIRE BUILT 2026-10-08 — option 1; option 2 scoped, not built] **LOCAL DEVELOPMENT REACHES PRODUCTION: ITS DATA, AND ITS PEOPLE.** `netlify dev` runs the functions with the live credentials. Measured under the real CLI (2026-10-08, probe function, credentials overridden for the run): besides the local `.env`, the LINKED SITE'S OWN SETTINGS hand local dev `S3_BUCKET`, `MY_AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `MY_AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `ABLY_ROOT_KEY`, `ONESIGNAL_APP_ID`, `ONESIGNAL_API_KEY` and the VAPID keys — so deleting `.env` would not have closed it. **THE CLASS IS WIDER THAN THE DELETION.** #453 is one instance: a work-in-progress autosave wrote Matrix's real data and removed 287 records. The same path, on any local edit that changes an assignment, sends a REAL push notification to a REAL phone (`diffTaskEvents` → OneSignal/web push) — a laptop experiment can wake someone up at home — and broadcasts over Ably to every production client; invites send real mail. **THE TRIPWIRE (option 1):** `netlify dev` always injects `NETLIFY_DEV=true` (netlify-cli `commands/dev/dev.js:60`; confirmed in the function process by the probe). `_utils/dev-guard.js` reads it at call time. Every S3 write REFUSES (`LocalDevWriteRefused`, 503): `s3.js`'s six mutators (`writeJson`, `writeJsonIfMatch`, `writeBinary`, `copyPrefix`, `copyObject`, `deleteObject`) and `backup-daily.js`'s own copy and prune; reads are allowed, so the local app loads. Ably publishes, OneSignal (`push.js` and the two direct fetches in `timeoff.js`, `notify.js`), web push and mail are no-ops. **CONSEQUENCE, BY DESIGN: local dev is now read-only against production — every save fails with a banner until option 2 exists.** It protects a machine only once that machine has the code. `scripts/dev-guard-test.mjs`: every case runs guard-on AND guard-off against patched SDK clients and fetch, so "nothing went out" is a count with a control beside it; red before wiring (14 failures, every guard-off control passing); 16 mutants, 16 caught (the prune guard's was MISSED at first — shadowed by the copy guard, which throws first — and a nothing-to-copy case was added for it). **OPTION 2, SCOPED NOT BUILT:** (a) a `traqs-dev` bucket, versioned, and an IAM user whose policy reaches only that bucket (no SES); (b) on the Netlify site, give the "Local development" context its own values — dev bucket, dev keys, empty Ably/OneSignal/VAPID/mail sender — so `netlify dev` can no longer receive production's; (c) strip production credentials from both machines' `.env` (this Mac's `~/traqs/.env`; the Windows `traqs-func/.env`) — and give the `scripts/measure-*` readers their OWN read-only IAM user, because today they read production with the same write-capable key; (d) a seed script for a synthetic dev org — `config.json`, `people.json` with the developer's login email as admin (membership is read from the bucket, so login depends on it), `settings.json`, `billing.json`, `tasks.json`, the org-code index entry, and a dev `PIN_PEPPER` the seeded PINs are hashed with (model: the #449 sandbox seed; check `scripts/seed-basic-org.mjs` first); (e) `dev-guard.js` then allows writes when `S3_BUCKET` is the dev bucket and keeps refusing the production one by name. Nothing in production depends on sharing; native apps point at the production URL regardless.

455. [DIALOG FIXED 2026-10-08 — behaviour deliberately unchanged] **SELECT → ALL → DELETE ON THE SCHEDULE PERMANENTLY REMOVES EVERY DRAWN BAR BEHIND ONE COUNT-ONLY CONFIRMATION.** A different path from #453, the same shape: one gesture, a whole set, physical removal. `All` (`TRAQS.jsx:15893`) selects every `type: "task"` bar on every person row in the visible window; Delete (`:31083`) filters those panel and op ids out of `subs` — removed from the file, not tombstoned, so no `deletedAt` trail. **THE CONFIRMATION DOES NOT NAME WHAT IS ABOUT TO GO:** "Delete N items? This will permanently remove the selected bars from the schedule. This action cannot be undone." — a count, no titles, no jobs, no people. **A TYPICAL SELECTION**, measured on Matrix's `tasks.json` 2026-10-08 with the schedule's draw rule (on someone's team, dated, not Finished, not history — approximated; the person-row filter was not applied): week view (Oct 5–11) **9 bars across 4 jobs**; month view **18 bars across 4 jobs** — which is EVERY task currently drawable from today forward. None carried logged hours at measurement; that is today's data, not a property of the path. The 287 history records restored in #453 are not reachable this way (history is not drawn). **THE FIX MAKES THE DIALOG HONEST, NOT THE GESTURE SAFER** (ruled: don't change the behaviour). It now reads "Delete N tasks from M jobs?", says the tasks are removed FROM THEIR JOBS (the old "from the schedule" understated it — they leave the file), notes any whole sub-jobs going, and lists every job with its task count and the task names. The list comes from the SAME walk the Delete uses — `src/barDelete.js`: `applyBarDelete` is the old inline filter moved verbatim (Set.has, panels then ops) and `planBarDelete` reports exactly what it removes — because selection ids are BAR ids: a selected panel takes its tasks with it, and an id matching no node removes nothing, so "what was selected" and "what goes" differ and the dialog says the second. Sandbox: Select → All on the synthetic org showed "Delete 3 tasks from 2 jobs?" with both jobs and their task names; Cancel left the file untouched; Delete removed exactly the three listed. `scripts/bar-delete-test.mjs`, 18 assertions (the old filter is kept in the suite as the reference the new one must equal); red on the unfixed dialog (6 failures); 8 mutants, 8 caught. STILL TRUE AND NOT ADDRESSED: one gesture can remove the whole forward schedule, physically, with no tombstone.

456. [FIXED 2026-10-08 — batched; the workflow half carried to v2] **THE GRID NEEDED THREE SAVES TO SCHEDULE A TASK; DROP IN SCHEDULE NEEDS ONE.** Measured in the #449 sandbox on the fixed code (after #450): from the Jobs grid a task reaches the schedule only through START (save), END (save), then ASSIGNEE (save) — the assignee cell refuses until dates exist ("Can't assign until dates are set."), so the order is forced. Drop in Schedule sets who and when in ONE save (`placeTaskAt` → `commitDates(node, { start, end, team })`). While #449 dropped every save after a session's first, the grid route could never complete — that was the visible failure; with #449 fixed all three saves land and the card draws, so the defect left is the shape itself: three round trips, two intermediate states written to the shared file (dated-unassigned, then dated-assigned), and three chances for a concurrent write or a stale tab to land between them. Also measured: Drop in Schedule is DISABLED for a task with no hours (`planNoHours`), and task hours can be entered only in Edit Job — the grid's HRS cell on a task row has no editor. **AND THE REFUSAL IS CLEAR BUT TERMINAL** (found 2026-10-08 in the Jobs/Schedule sweep, folded here rather than numbered separately — same shape, same weekend). The `+ Assign` popover does not merely refuse an undated task: `planUndated` renders a headed explanation — *Add dates first* — naming which date is missing and why it matters (*"Without a date range TRAQS cannot tell who is free, so everyone would look equally available"*). That is a refusal that says so, which is the bar most of this campaign has been about. What it does not do is offer any way to CLEAR the blocker: there is no date input in the popover, so the user is told precisely what is wrong and must leave, set two dates in two saves, and come back. The three saves are the shape; this is the moment the user meets it. **BATCHING IS POSSIBLE WITHOUT A REDESIGN — but it needs two rulings first.** What exists: each grid cell is independent today — `gridCell` holds ONE `{ id, col }` (`TRAQS.jsx:13741`), START/END commit at once through `commitCellEdit` → `commitDates(node, { [key]: val })` (`:12894`), and the assignee gate reads the COMMITTED `item.start`/`item.end` (`hasDates`, `:13956`). What makes it cheap: the combined write already exists and is proven — Drop in Schedule calls `commitDates(node, { start, end, team })`, one refusal chain, one moveLog entry, one save. So the change is contained: a per-row draft of `{ start, end, team }` held outside `tasks`; the three cells render the draft over the stored value; the assignee gate reads the draft's dates; and the third value commits all three through that one `commitDates`. Roughly one state, three cells and one commit point, plus a suite. RULINGS NEEDED BEFORE BUILDING: (a) a HALF-FILLED draft — when the user sets dates and walks away, are the dates saved on leaving the row (two saves, as today, but only then) or discarded? (b) SCOPE — batch only the unscheduled → scheduled case (an undated, unassigned task), and leave a single-field edit of an already-placed task as one immediate save? Recommended: yes to (b), and (a) save-on-leave so nothing typed is silently lost. Fits the weekend simplification as easily as now; it does not fight the architecture. **WORKFLOW FINDING, PLAINLY: THE FAST PATH HAS A PREREQUISITE THE SLOW PATH DOES NOT.** Drop in Schedule — the one-save route — is disabled for a task with no hours (`planNoHours`, `:28321`), and a task's hours can be entered only in Edit Job: the grid's HRS cell on a task row has no editor. So the one-save route costs a trip into a different page first (open the job, Edit, type hours, Save — itself a save), while the three-save grid route needs no hours at all. A new task made in Edit Job inherits its panel's hours, so the trip is avoided only when the panel already carries a sensible figure. **RULED 2026-10-08 AND BUILT.** (1) A half-filled draft is SAVED ON LEAVE — never dropped silently ("#449 wearing a different hat"). (2) Batching applies only when an unscheduled task is being scheduled; a single change to a placed task stays one immediate save. **THE FIX:** `src/schedDraft.js` holds the rules; on a leaf with neither both dates nor anybody on it, a START/END edit is HELD in a draft outside the tree (`commitCellEdit`), the cells show it (italic, accent, titled "Not saved yet"), the assignee gate and both busy checks read its dates, and picking who writes start, end and team in ONE `commitDates` — Drop in Schedule's own write. A refused completion keeps the draft. LEAVING saves the dates typed so far, in one write: any mousedown the drafted row does not claim (the row claims its own in `onMouseDownCapture`, which React routes through its portalled date picker and assignee menu), typing a date into another row, changing view, and closing the tab (which also asks first). **A BUG IN THE FIRST CUT, CAUGHT IN THE SANDBOX:** the click that MADE the draft (a day in the picker) left a stale claim, so the first click away was read as the row's own and the held date was never saved — exactly the silent loss ruling 1 forbids. The claim is now cleared in the capture phase, before React's handler, on every mousedown. **MEASURED** (sandbox, delta saves after a warm-up): START + END → 0 saves, dates shown; assignee → 1 save carrying `{ start, end, team }`; START then a click away → 1 save of the start. `scripts/grid-batch-test.mjs`, 35 assertions, wired; red on the unfixed code; 14 mutants, 14 caught — after two were MISSED on R4 shapes in the first draft (a bare `holdsDateEdit(` match that `if (false && …)` satisfied, and one regex covering both `assignPickerFor(_ad…)` sites). Two existing suites pinned the old shape and were updated with notes: `assign-cell-test.mjs` (the gate expression, now draft-aware) and `assign-consolidation-test.mjs` (a 160-character window between the START/END guard and `commitDates`, which the draft branch now sits inside — widened to 700; a placed task still lands on that call). **CARRIED TO v2, RULED A DESIGN PROBLEM NOT A DEFECT:** the one-save route needs hours, hours live only in Edit Job, so the fast path has a prerequisite the slow path does not — a user takes the three-save route because it is the one that does not send them somewhere else first. Batching makes that route safe; it does not remove the reason people take it.

457. [WEB FIXED AND PUSHED 2026-10-08 (0e3ad80); iOS FIXED, COMPILED, UNSHIPPED (7c3ddfb) — rides with #447's Mac build] **A SIGN-IN THAT OMITS THE ORG'S AUTH0 CONNECTION SHOWS A PAGE WITH NO MICROSOFT BUTTON.** Trey's partner could not sign in: Auth0 showed email/password and Google only. Matrix's `config.connection` is `"matrixpci"`, live `GET /org` returns it, and the `org-config` merge cannot remove it; a cached `tq_org_config` is not the cause either (the app re-fetches `/org` on every load). NOT #385: it never touched a sign-in call, and `login_hint` was never removed. The gap is the CALL: with `connection` set, Auth0 goes straight to Microsoft; without it, Auth0 shows its default page, where an enterprise connection has no button. **THE TWO PATHS THAT WORK PASS IT, THE TWO THAT DON'T, DON'T — AND NOBODY NOTICED BECAUSE THE WORKING PATHS ARE THE ONES WE USE.** Working: tapping your name on the web roster (`App.jsx:1914`) and the web admin sign-in (`:1922`). Broken: the web's session-RECOVERY re-login in `safeGetToken` (`:2066`, since 8bf6666, 2026-05-15), which only runs after a session expires — the path the partner hit — and the iPhone app's only sign-in, `auth.login()` with no arguments (`WelcomeView.swift:436`), which has never passed it. **FIXED:** both now pass the org's connection. Web: `scripts/login-connection-test.mjs`, a ratchet over every `loginWithRedirect` in `App.jsx` (the next sign-in added without it is this bug again) with must-catch/must-pass fixtures and an input guard; red on the unfixed code; 3 mutants, 3 caught. iOS: `auth.login(connection: org?.connection)`, `org` coming from `APIService.lookupOrg` (the public `/org`); xcodebuild BUILD SUCCEEDED, not run. **AUTH0 CHECKED BEFORE THE MAC BUILD:** the iOS app uses its OWN Auth0 client (not the web's), and `matrixpci` IS enabled for it — `/authorize` with the iOS `client_id`, its redirect URI and `connection=matrixpci` redirects straight to `login.microsoftonline.com/matrixpci.com`, identical to the web client; a nonexistent connection on the same client bounces to an Auth0 error resume instead, so the probe tells the two apart. The Swift fix alone is sufficient.

458. [INCIDENT 2026-10-08, RECOVERED SAME DAY] **#447 FIRED IN PRODUCTION SEVEN HOURS AFTER IT WAS LOGGED, AND IT WAS NOT A STATUS EDIT — IT WAS A PHOTO.** The clearest case this campaign has produced of a predicted defect firing, with the prediction, the trigger and the damage all on the record. THE CHAIN: **22:58Z 2026-10-07** — #447 logged, from reading the Swift: iOS types `status` as a five-case enum, decodes anything else as `.notStarted`, and `saveJobs` re-encodes the WHOLE `[Job]` array on any write, so 38 of 64 jobs were one save from being flattened. The fix was written and committed the same evening, **uncompiled**, parked for a Mac build. **00:29:17Z 2026-10-08** — Max attached a photo to panel `401944-03 (5) E` from the old TestFlight build. **That single upload re-encoded all 65 jobs.** 39 statuses — `Shipped/ invoiced` ×16, `finished` ×12, `MTX FAT`, `C FAT`, `Crated`, `Boxed up `, `Procurement`, `Shipped not invoiced` — became `Not Started`, and the status vocabulary in use collapsed from 11 values to 3. The same write stripped `fromStartHour`, `toStartHour`, `fromEndHour`, `toEndHour`, `fromTeam`, `toTeam`, `fromHpd`, `toHpd`, `fromDepartments` and `toDepartments` from **283 of 285 moveLog entries**, because Swift's `MoveLogEntry` models a subset too — so the record of WHO a move reassigned and at WHAT HOUR went with it. The file shrank 21 KB. **THE LESSON, AND IT IS THE WHOLE POINT OF THIS ENTRY: IT WAS NOT A STATUS EDIT.** Nobody touched a status. Somebody uploaded a photograph. **Any write from a client that models a subset of a record re-encodes the whole record and silently drops what it does not know** — and the trigger is therefore ANY action that saves, not the action that looks related to the damage. **THIRD INSTANCE, THREE DIFFERENT FIELDS, ONE MECHANISM:** #384 lost two PINs and two push tokens and an active clock, #349 lost approved PTO, this lost 39 statuses and 283 moveLog histories. Each was investigated as its own defect because each presented as a different symptom. `JSONPassthrough.swift` exists to solve exactly this for keys Swift does not model; `status` and the moveLog's hour fields fall outside it because they ARE modelled, just too narrowly. **A narrower type is the same bug as a missing field, and the passthrough cannot see it.** RECOVERY, 2026-10-08, two guarded writes, both `IfMatch` on the ETag read at plan time so a concurrent save would have refused rather than clobbered. **NOT A FILE ROLLBACK** — the damaging write also carried Max's photo, which a rollback would have deleted, so both passes restored FIELD BY FIELD keyed on id from version `_G1jj8FEpnGatFFaEEfQHIMnXVy0bsgB` (22:58:16Z). Pass one: 39 statuses, with `fields other than status that this plan would change: 0` asserted before writing. Pass two: 283 moveLog entries refilled, matched BY POSITION — the flatten re-encoded each entry in place so order survived, which is the only way to tell two identical drags apart; an identity-keyed match left 125 ambiguous and position left none. Verified from S3 after each: **65 live jobs, 11 distinct statuses, Not Started back to 3, 283 entries carrying hour detail, 16 attachments** (Max's upload intact). WHAT THE CASCADE TURNED OUT TO BE, since "the rest of the diff" is where damage hides: `loggedHours`, `actualHours`, `team`, `start`, `end`, `startHour`, `endHour`, `hpd`, `pri`, `deps`, `clientId` and every date were **byte-identical across all 582 nodes**; 582 nodes before and after, none missing; one job lost a `dueDate`. The 20 moveLogs, 18 placedSubs, 16 finishRequests, 4 apprLogs, 3 attachments, 3 engineering and 1 signOffs that "changed" were key REORDERING by Swift's encoder, proved by comparing canonically rather than by eye. **TIMELINE NOTE WORTH KEEPING: the fix existed, committed, before the damage happened.** What was missing was a build. An uncompiled fix protects nobody, and the gap between "correct in the repo" and "correct on the device" is measured in whoever opens the app next.

459. [FOUND 2026-10-08 in the Jobs/Schedule sweep] **#450'S CLIP, TWICE MORE, IN THE TWO SITES ITS OWN ENTRY LEFT UNMEASURED.** `cellBase` — the Jobs grid's cell style — carries `overflow: "hidden"` (`TRAQS.jsx:13752`), which is what made #450's START/END/DUE calendar invisible and unwritable. Inside it, four lines apart in the same `customCols.map`: the **select** cell (`:14318`) opens through `placePopover(r, …)`, a measured fixed-position popover that escapes the clip; the **date** cell (`:14331`) renders `<DateField square compact>` INLINE with no `portal`. A second instance at `:13666`, in the job-detail panel, where its `SimpleDrop` sibling IS `pill portal` and the `DateField` beside it is not. `DateField` has supported `portal` since `:2789` and five call sites pass it (`:9854`, `:23797`, `:25568` and the two Settings holiday pickers). #450's entry names exactly these as left over — *"`:14243`/`:13596` remain UNMEASURED (same row container, plausibly the same clip)"* — and warns that an earlier fix was applied to the wrong component, which is why the sites here are given by their current line numbers AND by what distinguishes them: the custom-column cells reached through `startEdit(e, item.id, key)`, not the three fixed-column pickers. **MEASURED: the container clips (source). INFERRED: that the calendar is therefore invisible** — inferred from #450's browser measurement of the identical container, not re-measured, because this machine has no browser. **FIXED 2026-10-08:** both take the picker's existing `portal` mode, the same one-word fix #450 applied to the three fixed-column cells. `scripts/grid-date-portal-test.mjs` gains a fourth section asserting both, that no `square` `DateField` is left unportaled, that the select cell's `placePopover` escape still stands, and that `cellBase` still clips — because the day that stops being true, the premise of all of it is gone and the assertions should say so rather than quietly pass.

460. [FOUND 2026-10-08 in the sweep] **THE FINISH-APPROVAL CARD READS 2.8% OF THE HOURS IT CLAIMS TO, AND IT IS THE NUMBER AN ADMIN APPROVES AGAINST.** Four surfaces compute "hours on this op" from `timeclock` rows whose `jobRefs` mention it: `:21741` and `:22375` show `{h} logged` on the mobile and desktop Finish Requests cards, and `:22125` and `:22376` resolve `lastWorker` — the PERSON named on the card. **`jobRefs` WAS NEVER THE RIGHT SOURCE FOR THIS, AND THE CODEBASE ALREADY SAYS SO.** `finishedOpFields` carries #188's note verbatim: *"this read PAYROLL entries — `timeclock` rows whose jobRefs mention the op — and summed their `hours`, which is the whole SHIFT, counted once per job the worker picked at clock-in … an op worked for 2h inside an 8h shift was recorded as having taken 8."* #188 fixed the WRITE and left four READS. So this is the fifth implementation of "hours on an op", and it is the one with a button next to it. **MEASURED ON LIVE DATA:** `timeclock.json` has 177 rows of which **5** carry a non-empty `jobRefs`, reaching **4** ops and **30.59 h**; `productionhours.json` has 270 rows, **55** ops, **1,109.54 h**. The card can therefore see **2.8%** of the hours that exist. Across every op with hours from either source the two formulas disagree **31 of 31 — 100%** — the card reads **0.00h** where the rest of the app reads 5.16h, 24.00h, **53.03h**. It has two failure modes and neither is the right answer: `jobRefs` empty (172 of 177 rows) gives ZERO, `jobRefs` populated gives THE WHOLE SHIFT. The `lastWorker` pair fails the same way — `undefined` for nearly every op, so `{worker?.name || job.title}` falls back to the JOB TITLE and the card does not say who asked. **UNEXERCISED TODAY, NOT LATENT:** 0 ops currently have `pendingFinish: true`, but the key is present on 23, so the first request from iOS lands on this. **FIXED 2026-10-08.** All four surfaces take the one shared answer: `_opHoursPair(op).logged` for the hours — the canonical implementation, which already carried the reasoning (*"Logged = JOB-clock time recorded against THIS op — NOT payroll hours"*) — and a new `lastWorkerFor(op)` for the person, backed by `lastWorkedByScope` in `statsMath.js` beside `producedHoursByScope`, with the SAME op→panel→job fallback, because a panel-level clock-in has a null `opId` and would otherwise name nobody. Ordered by when the work ENDED, with a session still running counted as the most recent rather than as the epoch. **WHAT WAS DELIBERATELY NOT SWEPT:** `jobRefs` remains correct for what it actually means — the live-status chips and the timesheet rows listing which jobs a person picked at clock-in — and the suite asserts those survive, because a blanket removal would have taken them with it. `scripts/finish-approval-hours-test.mjs`, 19 assertions, inside an 18-mutant run with 0 survivors.

461. [FOUND 2026-10-08 in the sweep; TRACE COMPLETE] **A SCHEDULING PREVIEW IS PERSISTED, IS 15.6% OF `tasks.json`, AND NOTHING EVER READS IT.** `placedSubs` is replan scratch. `_bsClean` strips it (`:25087`) — but only into `_bsProbe`, the overlap-check probe. The written path is `newSubs` (`:25010`), whose non-`_panelScheduled` branch returns `{...op, …}` and `op` still carries it. **MEASURED: 18 panels store it, and 62 of its entries disagree with the real op** — wrong dates, and one entry naming an op that no longer exists (`ddfasdfadsf/Op-001`: placed `2026-09-08` vs real `2026-09-10`; placed op `tf0e8zlah` gone). **It costs 107,162 bytes — 15.6% of the file** — carried on every read and, before #339, every write. **THE TRACE IS FINISHED AND THE ANSWER IS DEAD WEIGHT, NOT A LIVE HAZARD:** `resultSubs = expandedOps.map(op => ({ ...op, placedSubs: (op.subs||[]).map(…) }))` (`:24943`) rebuilds `placedSubs` FRESH on every run, overwriting whatever arrived via `...op`, so the read at `:25015` can never see a stored copy. No other reader exists. WHY THE SCRATCH CONVENTION MISSED IT: `stripDerived` keys on the `_derived` marker and the run-local convention is a leading underscore (`_outcome`, `_placed`, both correctly absent from S3). **`placedSubs` has no underscore**, so neither guard applies — the convention is the whole protection and the name is outside it. **FIXED 2026-10-08 AT THE SOURCE.** `newSubs` now destructures `placedSubs` out rather than spreading `...op`, and strips `_placed`/`_outcome` from each sub on the way, so a new write cannot carry any of it. **THE 18 EXISTING PANELS ARE NOT CLEANED UP HERE** — that is a production write and wants its own guarded pass like #446's. They are inert until then, costing 107 KB on every read. **CLEANED UP 2026-10-08, AND THE TWO NUMBERS IN THIS ENTRY WERE PAIRED WRONGLY.** "18 panels" counted LIVE nodes; "107,162 bytes" was the TOTAL including soft-deleted jobs. Both were measured correctly and reported as though they described the same set. The real split: **18 live panels = 16,417 bytes**, and **230 further panels under 49 soft-deleted jobs = 90,745 bytes**. `scripts/cleanup-scratch-residue.mjs`, dry-run by default and guarded on the ETag, removed the **live** scope only — 18 `placedSubs` plus #462's 4 `_rescheduleStartDate`, 22 keys, 686,290 -> 669,477 bytes. Verified from S3 afterwards: 0 live occurrences, 65 live jobs, 440 moveLog entries unchanged. The 231 under deleted jobs were deliberately LEFT, because #473 makes cleaning them probably wasted work. The script refuses unless everything other than those two exact key names is byte-identical on both sides, and a live-scope run additionally refuses if it left a live occurrence behind or touched anything under a deleted job.

462. [FOUND 2026-10-08 in the sweep] **MODAL DRAFT STATE IN THE STORED TREE.** `_rescheduleStartDate` — the schedule modal's local date input (`ed._rescheduleStartDate`, `:24588`) — is persisted on **4 nodes**, 170 bytes. Same shape as #461 by a different route, and the opposite lesson: this one DOES carry the underscore, so the convention names it correctly and still nothing strips it. Together they say the scratch-field convention is documentation, not a mechanism — there is no single place that removes run-local state before a write, only `_bsClean` for one probe and `stripDerived` for `_derived`-marked fields. **FIXED 2026-10-08 BY MAKING THE CONVENTION A MECHANISM.** `stripDerived` now drops every leading-underscore key on the way to S3, so run-local state cannot reach it merely by being forgotten at one call site. **`_cc_<uuid>` IS THE EXCEPTION AND IS REAL DATA** — custom-column values are stored under dynamic underscore keys, which is exactly why this could not simply drop everything beginning with one — and the suite asserts they survive at job and panel level. **CLEANED UP 2026-10-08** in the same guarded write as #461's 18 — 4 live jobs (401944 - Thacker II, 402055, 402056, 402057), 48 bytes. A fifth sits under a soft-deleted job and was left with #461's 230.

463. [FOUND 2026-10-08 in the sweep; NOT BUILT, awaiting a production env value] **TWO IMPLEMENTATIONS OF "WHAT MAY A STALE CLIENT OVERWRITE", DISAGREEING.** `people.js` pins four fields (`activeClockIn`, `activeJobClock`, `activeBreak`, `pushToken`) AND carries an absence rule (`timeOff` absent ≠ cleared). `tasks.js` pins **one**: `SERVER_OWNED_FIELDS = ["loggedHours"]`, with no absence rule. `timeclock.js` writes `pendingFinish` and `finishRequests` onto ops — the iOS finish-request path — and neither is pinned, so a whole-tree POST from a stale client can destroy a worker's open request. What stands between them is the per-job stale check, and **`ruleMode()` DEFAULTS TO `"log"`**, which records the conflict and lets the write through. The local `.env` says `TASK_CONFLICT_MODE=enforce`; the production value lives in Netlify's dashboard and is **not readable from this machine**. #185 already names `status`, `pendingFinish` and the finish-request list as exactly what `enforce` would cover and `#323` does not. So this is either already mitigated or live, decided by a value nobody has read — and NOT BUILT AGAINST, on Trey's instruction, rather than guessed at. **ANSWERED 2026-10-08: `TASK_CONFLICT_MODE` IS `enforce` IN PRODUCTION, so this is MITIGATED — and the mitigation is a FLAG, not a line of code.** The per-job stale check keeps the stored copy and reports the id back, which covers `status`, `pendingFinish` and the finish-request list that `SERVER_OWNED_FIELDS` does not. **ANYONE WHO SETS THAT VARIABLE BACK TO `log` REOPENS THIS WITHOUT EDITING ANYTHING**, and `ruleMode()` defaults to `log` when the variable is absent, so losing it is the same as turning it off. The asymmetry in the code is unchanged and still worth closing on its own terms — `people.js` pins four fields and an absence rule, `tasks.js` pins one — because a guard that depends on an environment variable nobody can see from the repository is a guard whose state has to be asked about rather than read.


465. [FOUND 2026-10-08 while explaining the rule-event log gap] **EVERY SERVER-SIDE INSTRUMENT RAN ON THE WRITE THAT CAUSED #458, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM FOUND NOTHING.** Max's iOS save at 00:29:17Z flattened 39 job statuses and stripped the hour and team fields from 283 moveLog entries, and it left **no record at all** — `rule-events.json` has not been written since 2026-10-06T14:55:03Z. Nothing failed: `recordRuleEvents` returns early on an empty array, and `durable` WAS empty, because the write tripped none of the five things anything watches. **WHAT EACH INSTRUMENT ACTUALLY WATCHES, and why none of them saw it:** `task-conflict` watches STALENESS, and his copy was not stale — proven rather than assumed, because `TASK_CONFLICT_MODE` is `enforce` in production, so a stale job would have kept its stored copy and its status would not have moved; all 39 moved. `server-owned-field` watches `loggedHours` and nothing else. `schedule-rule` emits exactly two rules, `activeClock` (an op someone is clocked into was removed) and `department`, reading `.team` and `.start`. `overlap-rule` watches one person doing two things at once. `permission-gate` watches two classifiers disagreeing. **`scheduleRules.js` CONTAINS ZERO OCCURRENCES OF `.status` OR `moveLog`.** So the instruments are live, they ran, and they are blind to the failure mode that actually occurred — which is a different and worse thing than an instrument that is dead, because a dead one eventually gets noticed. **WHAT THIS DOES AND DOES NOT DO TO THE THREE ZEROS.** It does not weaken them on their own terms: `schedule-rule`'s zero still means nobody removed a clocked-into op in 279 writes, `overlap-rule`'s still means nobody was double-booked, `permission-gate`'s still means the classifiers agreed 279 times. None of them ever claimed to watch status or move history. What it does say is that **flipping all three would not have prevented #458, and nothing currently would** — the backstop that exists for dates, teams, overlap and permissions has no counterpart for the two fields a narrowing client destroyed. **NOT PROPOSED, because the obvious guard is wrong:** `status` cannot join `SERVER_OWNED_FIELDS`, since a user changing a status is the normal case and pinning it would break the product. The shape that would catch it is a BULK-CHANGE heuristic — one write moving 39 of 65 statuses to the same value is suspicious in a way one write moving one is not — and that is a rule nobody has designed, with a false-positive cost (a legitimate bulk edit) that has to be weighed before anyone builds it. **BUILT 2026-10-08 — THE NARROW RULE, BECAUSE THE GENERAL ONE IS NOT EXPRESSIBLE.** Tested rather than argued: field-disappearance, the obvious general rule, would have caught **ONE key** of #458's damage — a single `dueDate`. The statuses were **0 removed, 40 changed in place**, and the moveLog loss was inside array ELEMENTS, not node keys. It is also unusable generically here, because absence is already load-bearing in three places by design: `mergeTimeOff` exists *because* absent ≠ cleared, #339's delta means absent = unchanged, and iOS's `AbsentOnDecode` deliberately omits `hpd` so an unestimated op does not come back as 7.5 — the client most likely to trip the rule is the one relying on it. And "narrowed to a smaller set" needs the CLIENT's value set, which the server never sees: "Not Started" is a legal member of the org's seventeen, and nothing in the request separates *the user chose this* from *my enum could not say anything else*. **SO THE RULE IS ABOUT MOVE HISTORY ONLY, and the measurement made it tight.** Across #458's write: 343 entry pairs, **281 strict subsets** (fields dropped, none changed), **0 values changed**, **0 arrays shortened**, and **no other field lost a single key** — `finishRequests`, `apprLog`, `attachments`, `engineering`, `signOffs`, `placedSubs`, `deps` and `team` all clean. The `engineering`/`signOffs` differences an earlier pass flagged were **key reordering with identical values**, not loss. THE RULE: for a node that already exists in storage, every stored entry must survive the write intact — an incoming entry may ADD fields, never drop or change them; the array may GROW, never shrink. `keepMoveLogDetail` in `_utils/move-log-guard.js`, applied beside `keepServerOwned` on the same index, with one `move-log-detail` record per repaired entry so the volume finally says how often a narrowing client is rewriting history. **NO FALSE POSITIVES, checked against every write path rather than assumed:** each is `[...(existing || []), newEntry]`, a pure append; the one exception is `dragMove.js`'s split writing `moveLog: [goLog]` onto a BRAND-NEW node with a new id, which has no stored counterpart and is never examined. **AND IT DOES NOT ASK iOS TO MODEL MORE** — a thinner entry a client APPENDS passes untouched, because only entries that already exist are protected. It stops a client rewriting history without requiring it to understand history. Matched BY POSITION, the same choice the recovery made: the re-encode rewrote each entry in place so order held, and two identical drags produce entries whose every surviving field is equal, which no content key can tell apart (identity matching left 125 ambiguous; position left none). **WHAT IT DOES NOT CLOSE, and this is written down so nobody later assumes the backstop is wider than it is: THE 39 FLATTENED STATUSES.** A status is not pinnable — a user changing one is the normal case — and no server-side rule can tell "the user chose Not Started" from "my enum could not say anything else", because both arrive as a legal member of the org's own list. **The only defences there are the iOS fix (#447) and whoever installs it.** Of the three instances this mechanism has produced, this rule closes #458's move history completely, #349's PTO was already closed by `mergeTimeOff`, and #384's PINs could never have been reached by it — that record found no `stored` at all, and `String(p.id)` was the right answer. `scripts/movelog-immutable-test.mjs`, 32 assertions, **the red proof is #458's own write** replayed from `fixtures/movelog-458.json` — the real arrays from both S3 versions, 17 nodes and 283 entries, of which 281 arrive with no hour detail at all and all 281 come back. 12 mutants, 12 caught. Two survived the first run: one was a NO-OP of mine (`storedLog[i] ?? m` past the end of the array gives `m` back), and the other found **dead code in the guard itself** — an `|| !Array.isArray(n.moveLog)` that could never be the deciding term, since `mine` is `[]` in every such case and the short-array test already covers it. Removed rather than kept.


466. [FOUND 2026-10-08, sweep — the duplicate path] **A DUPLICATED JOB IS BORN CARRYING MOVE HISTORY THAT NEVER HAPPENED TO IT.** `duplicateJob` (`src/jobDetail.js`) is careful where it looks: fresh ids throughout, deps remapped onto them, status back to Not Started, the job number cleared so two jobs never share one, and a DROP list of ten fields that record work done — `loggedHours`, `actualHours`, `finishRequest`, `finishRequests`, `attachments`, `signOffs`, `engineering`, `apprChain`, `apprComments`, `apprLog`. **`moveLog` IS NOT ON THAT LIST**, and neither is `placedSubs`, `isReschedule`, `scheduledLater` or `finishedAt`. MEASURED ON THE LIVE BOARD, not reasoned about: 37 nodes carry a moveLog, 343 entries in total, and **16 of 65 jobs would hand their copy at least one**. Duplicating 402057 today produces a job born with **201 moveLog entries** — "moved by Max from 8am to 2pm, team was X" — about moves that happened to the original. 18 panels carry `placedSubs`, whose contents are OLD ids that `remap` never touches because remap is applied to `deps` alone. 4 jobs carry `_rescheduleStartDate` and 4 carry `isReschedule`, so the copy claims to be a reschedule of something. **AND #465 NOW MAKES THIS PERMANENT — an honest consequence of my own fix.** The copy has a new id, so `keepMoveLogDetail` finds no stored counterpart and lets the false history through on the first save; from the SECOND save onward that history is stored, and the append-only rule defends it as though it were real. Before #465 a narrowing client would at least have thinned it. The guard cannot tell invented history from earned history, and nothing asked it to. `finishedAt` sits on 1 live op (Salares Norte) and all three copy paths carry it, so the copy gets a Not Started op with a finish timestamp.

467. [FOUND 2026-10-08, sweep — the template path] **TEMPLATE SAVE DROPS NOTHING THAT RECORDS WORK, AND TEMPLATE LOAD DOES NOT REMAP DEPS.** The save (`TRAQS.jsx`, the Save-template modal) spreads `{...o}` and clears FIVE SCHEDULE FIELDS — `start`, `end`, `team`, `status`, `qty` — under a comment that says "cleared of stale schedule data". It is accurate about schedule data and silent about everything else: `loggedHours`, `moveLog`, `finishRequests`, `attachments`, `signOffs`, `apprLog` and `finishedAt` all ride into the template, and `loadTemplate` spreads them onto the new op. On the live board 31 ops carry `loggedHours` and 14 carry a `finishRequests` list, so a template captured from a worked panel is a template of somebody's hours. **MEASUREMENT BLOCKED, AND SAYING SO RATHER THAN GUESSING: templates live in `localStorage` under `tq_templates_${orgCode}`, per browser.** They are not in S3, so I cannot say how many templates exist, who has them, or whether any was captured from a worked panel — only that the path carries it if one was. That storage choice is itself worth noting: a "template" nobody else on the team can see. **AND THE DEPS BUG, which is not conditional on any of that.** Both `loadTemplate`s mint fresh ids with `uid()` and then write `deps: o.deps || []` verbatim, so every dependency still points at the template's own op ids, which are not on this board. `duplicateJob` remaps deps and gets this right; the template path does not. **THERE ARE TWO `loadTemplate`s** — `:24190` on the overlay wizard (`modal.type === "edit"`) and `:31516` on the desktop page (`editJobModal`) — and they disagree about feedback: the desktop one calls `markNew(...newIds)` and toasts `Template "X" loaded`, the overlay one does neither, so on that surface ops appear with no toast and no new-row highlight. Same bug in both, different amount of evidence that anything happened.

468. [FOUND 2026-10-08, sweep — the split path] **THE SPLIT STRIPS THE DEPRECATED FINISH POINTER AND KEEPS THE AUTHORITATIVE ONE.** `applySplit` (`src/dragMove.js`) builds the new half by destructuring four fields off the original — `const { actualHours, pendingFinish, pendingSession, finishRequest, ...base } = orig` — then zeroes `loggedHours`, clears `deps`, writes a fresh `moveLog: [goLog]` and records `splitFrom: orig.id`. That is careful work, and it removes **`finishRequest`, SINGULAR**, which `src/finishRequests.js` describes as no longer written by anything. It keeps **`finishRequests`, PLURAL**, which the same file calls the authoritative list. So the half nobody requested inherits the original's finish-request history, and by `pendingFinishOf` rule 1 — "a pending entry exists → OPEN", which needs no mirror and therefore does not care that `pendingFinish` was stripped — a copied pending entry reopens the request on the new half. `attachments`, `signOffs`, `engineering`, `apprLog` and `finishedAt` ride along in `base` too. **LIVE EXPOSURE TODAY IS ONE OP.** 358 of 394 ops pass the context item's own gate (`hpd > 1` and not Finished); of those, exactly **1** carries a `finishRequests` list, and **0** entries anywhere on the board are still `"pending"`. So this is latent, not realised — it fires when someone splits an op whose request is open, which is an ordinary thing to do.

**THE SHAPE UNDERNEATH #466, #467 AND #468: THREE PATHS COPY A NODE AND NO TWO AGREE ON WHAT "WORK DONE" MEANS.** `duplicateJob` drops ten and remaps deps. `applySplit` drops four, zeroes one, clears deps, resets the log. Template save/load drops five SCHEDULE fields and no work field at all. Measured against the live board, by field: `loggedHours` (31 ops) — duplicate drops, split drops, template CARRIES. `finishRequests` (14 ops) — duplicate drops, split CARRIES, template CARRIES. `moveLog` (34 ops) — duplicate CARRIES, split drops, template CARRIES. `finishedAt` (1 op) — all three CARRY. Not one field is treated the same way by all three. This is the same shape as the narrowing-client family (#384/#349/#458): not three bugs that happen to rhyme, but one missing idea — there is no single answer anywhere to "what does a copy of this node inherit", so each path invented its own and each got a different subset right. `scripts/measure-copy-paths.mjs` prints the matrix. **NOT PROPOSED, because the fix is a design decision rather than a patch:** the obvious move is one exported `WORK_RECORD_FIELDS` that all three strip, but split deliberately keeps some of what duplicate drops (it is the same work continuing, not a new job), so the right answer may be two lists rather than one, and that is Trey's call.

469. [FOUND 2026-10-08, sweep — the AI path] **THE AI REPORTS A JOB EDIT IT DID NOT MAKE, AND IT IS #436'S DEFECT IN THE SIBLING POSITION.** `assign_person_to_job` and `remove_person_from_job` each decide whether they applied by calling `jobExists(input.job_id)`, which is `tasks.some(t => sameId(t.id, id))` — LOOSE, written that way on purpose. The mutation on the very next line is `prev.map(t => t.id === input.job_id ? ... : t)` — STRICT. `input.job_id` arrives from the MODEL, so its type is whatever the tool call emitted, which is exactly the provenance #436 called out when it fixed `input.person_id` on those same two lines with `withPerson`/`withoutPerson`. The person id was fixed and the JOB id on the same expression was left strict. A type-drifted `job_id` passes the loose existence check, matches nothing in the strict map, and `outcomes[tu.id] = changed ? OUTCOME_APPLIED : OUTCOME_NOCHANGE` records APPLIED while the board is unchanged and the user is told **"✓ Changes applied successfully."** Two sites, `:11174` and `:11178`. MARKED AS INFERRED where it matters: I have not observed the model emitting a numeric `job_id` — live ids are all strings (582 of 582) — so what is proven is the asymmetry between the two comparisons, not a sighting of it firing.

470. [FOUND 2026-10-08, sweep — the AI path] **`delete_job` REPORTS SUCCESS WHEN THE DELETE WAS REFUSED.** `changed` is initialised to `true` at the top of the loop and every other case overwrites it; `case "delete_job"` calls `delTask(input.job_id)` and breaks without touching it. `delTask` opens with `if (blockedByActiveClock(jobIdOfNode(id))) return;` — a silent early return that raises the "Worker Clocked Into Job" overlay and deletes nothing. The AI path never sees that refusal, so the outcome is recorded APPLIED and the user is told the change went through. `create_job` inherits the same default and is harmless, since a create cannot be refused that way. The honest statement of scope: this is one line, and the fix is to have `delTask` report whether it acted rather than to have the caller guess.

471. [FOUND 2026-10-08, sweep — sorting] **SORTING BY PRIORITY SORTS BY THE ORDER SOMEBODY ADDED THE OPTIONS IN SETTINGS.** The Jobs-list comparator keys `pri` as `PRI_ORDER.indexOf(t.pri || "Medium")`, and `PRI_ORDER` is `priOpts.map(o => o.name)` — the org's DISPLAY list. Matrix's is `["On time", "Medium", "Late", "Low", "High"]`, so ascending gives On time → Medium → Late → Low → High, which is not a priority order in either direction: Low sorts above Medium, and High sorts below both. The list also conflates two vocabularies — schedule state (On time, Late) with severity (Low, Medium, High) — and reuses colours across them: On time and Low are both `#10b981`, Late and High are both `#f43f5e`, so two chips that mean different things are painted the same. **REALISED IMPACT TODAY IS ZERO AND THE ENTRY SAYS SO:** only two values are in use on the board, Medium (64 jobs) and High (1), which happen to sort correctly relative to each other. It becomes visible the first time anyone sets a job to Low. **THE RELATED `indexOf` HAZARD IS CLEAN, MEASURED NOT ASSUMED:** both `status` and `pri` key through `indexOf`, which returns **-1** for a value outside the list and therefore sorts an unrecognised value AHEAD of everything. Against live data, 0 of 65 jobs and 0 of 582 nodes carry a status or priority outside the org list — #446's normalisation is holding — so the -1 is a landmine with nothing on it yet rather than a defect today.

472. [FOUND 2026-10-08, sweep — visual] **A COMPLETE THEME NO USER CAN SELECT, AND A QUARTER OF THE CONTRAST SUITE IS SPENT ON IT.** `obsidian` is defined in full at `TRAQS.jsx:2449` — bg, surface, card, border, borderLight, text, the lot — and carries its own CSS at `:2112` for date-input pickers. It appears in `scripts/contrast-test.mjs`'s `THEMES` ladder alongside midnight, frost and custom(Matrix). **IT IS OFFERED BY NO PICKER.** Both theme segment controls, `:26756` and `:27545`, list exactly `frost` ("Light") and `midnight` ("Dark"). Three occurrences of the string in the whole file and not one of them is a control. The suite is green — 2904 assertions, 0 failed — and roughly a quarter of those assertions measure a ladder nobody can reach, which inflates what the green means without any of it being wrong. Either the theme should be selectable or it should stop costing a quarter of the contrast budget; both are reasonable and the choice is Trey's. MARKED AS INFERRED: a user whose stored preference predates the picker losing the option would still render obsidian, and I have not checked whether any stored pref holds it.

**THE SWEEP'S NON-FINDINGS, recorded because a quiet answer is still an answer.** (a) **EVERY DELETE ENTRY POINT IS PERMISSION-GATED.** Five controls set `confirmDelete`/`confirmDelTask`/`bulkDeleteConfirm`: `:22500` and `:22603` under `can("editJobs")`, `:25227` the same, `:25666` under `canEdit`, `:30493` under `can("editJobs")`, the jobs bulk under `jobSelectMode` which is itself behind `can("editJobs")` at `:13423`, and the clients bulk under `can("manageClients")`. I first read `:22500` as ungated and it is not — the gate is on the wrapping `<div>` at `:22498`, one line up. (b) **THE DELETE CASCADE'S ORPHANS ARE A WORKING DENORMALISATION, NOT A LEAK.** 133 of 270 production-hour records (49.3%), carrying 380 of 1110 hours, point at a job, panel or op id that is no longer in the tree. Every one of the 133 still carries its `jobTitle` and 108 carry an `opTitle`, so the row still reads; `producedHoursByScope` keys by id, so those hours are simply never looked up by a live job rather than misattributed to one; and person-day totals legitimately include hours somebody worked on a job since deleted. No surface shows a wrong number. The first run of this measurement read `person.jobSessions` and reported **0 sessions scanned**, which looked like "nothing is orphaned" — sessions live in `orgs/{org}/productionhours.json`, which is deliberately not a /sync entity. A zero from the wrong object is a false negative that reads exactly like a clean result. (c) **`delTask` COMPARES IDS RAW** — `n.id !== id` and `d !== id` — which is the person-id-drift shape in a place no ratchet watches; live data is 582 of 582 string node ids and 21 of 21 string dep entries, and all 21 deps are within a single job, so there is nothing to catch today and nothing enforcing that tomorrow. (d) `pendingFinish` sits on 23 ops as a KEY and on **0** as the value `true`; a scan that counts keys reports 23 and is wrong by 23, which is why #466's and #468's finish-request claims are written as latent rather than live. (e) **THE BUILD'S ONE DUPLICATE-KEY WARNING IS DEAD CODE, NOT A BUG.** `vite:esbuild` reports `Duplicate key "cursor"` at `TRAQS.jsx:28496`: the Drop-in-Schedule button writes `cursor: "pointer"` on one line and `cursor: planNoHours ? "not-allowed" : "pointer"` five lines later. The last key wins in a JS object literal, so the CORRECT one is live and the first is simply overwritten. The button itself is the best-guarded control I read all sweep — `disabled={planNoHours}`, an early `if (planNoHours) return;`, the border, background, colour, opacity and label all switching on the same flag, and the hover handlers refusing to paint. Worth deleting the dead line so the warning stops, but it changes nothing, and a reorder is the only thing that would make it matter.


473. [FOUND 2026-10-08 while scoping #461's cleanup] **60.5% OF `tasks.json` IS JOBS THAT WERE DELETED.** 49 of 114 top-level jobs carry `deletedAt` and are still in the file: **415,265 of 686,290 bytes**. The 65 live jobs are 271,026. This came out of reconciling #461's own mismatched pair — `placedSubs` is on 18 live panels worth 16 KB and on 230 dead ones worth 91 KB, and chasing why led to the much larger number sitting underneath it. **SO THE "107 KB OF DEAD WEIGHT ON EVERY READ" FRAMING WAS A TENTH OF THE ACTUAL WEIGHT, AND SAYING SO IS THE POINT OF THIS ENTRY.** Oldest deletion 2026-07-03, newest 2026-10-02; by age, 35 within 30 days, 11 at 30-90 days, 3 at 90-180, none older. Every one has a timestamp, so a retention rule has something to key on. **NOT PROPOSED AND NOT ACTED ON.** Soft delete is presumably what makes a delete recoverable, and nothing in the campaign has established how long that needs to hold, whether anything reads a deleted job today (the Jobs list filters them out; the production-hours rows that reference them are matched by id, not by tree position), or whether purging should archive rather than drop. That is a product decision about retention, not a defect. What is defensible without deciding any of it: cleaning scratch fields out of records that may be purged is work with a short life, which is why #461's cleanup took the live scope and left the rest.

**THE COPY-PATH RULE, MEASURED BEFORE PROPOSING (#466 + #468).** Trey's instinct — *anything that records what happened to the original stays behind; anything that describes the work itself comes along* — is the right axis and survives contact with the data in most places. Where it does not, measured on live nodes: **(1) `notes` IS ON THE WRONG SIDE, and it is the biggest single miss.** The name says description; the content is a running progress log. 62 of 65 live jobs carry one and they read `"PB. Status: Crated. Contact Riley. 100%. CFAT. Change order request"`, `"Status: Shipped. Contact Mandy."`, `"Still waiting for equipment."` All three copy paths carry `notes` today, so a duplicate of 402026 is born announcing `Status: Shipped. 100%.` **(2) CUSTOM COLUMNS CANNOT BE CLASSIFIED BY A LIST AT ALL.** The org defines them and adds more whenever it likes; there are 7 today and they fall on both sides by construction — `"Act Hrs"` is `type=acthours` and `"Activity"` is `type=activity`, both work records by definition, while `"Category"` (`select`, values "PB"/"Support", on 18 jobs) is pure description and `"Comments"` (`text`, on 5 jobs) holds `"Rittal next Wednesday"` — a record wearing a descriptive name. Any hardcoded DROP list is stale the moment somebody adds a column, so the classification has to key on the column's TYPE from settings, or each column needs its own "copies?" flag. **(3) THERE IS A THIRD CATEGORY THE TWO BUCKETS HAVE NO ROOM FOR: COMMERCIAL IDENTITY.** `jobNumber` (63 jobs) is already cleared by `duplicateJob` precisely so two jobs never share one — and `poNumber` (53 jobs) and `dueDate` (50 jobs) are the same kind of thing and are both carried. They neither record work nor describe it; they identify the engagement. Two jobs sharing a PO number is a billing problem. **(4) SPLIT IS NOT A COPY AND SHOULD NOT TAKE THE SAME ANSWER.** Its new half is the same work continuing, which is why zeroing `loggedHours` is right — but `deps: []` throws away predecessor constraints the continuation still has, and deps sit in the "comes along" bucket, so the rule as stated does not predict that loss. 8 live ops carry deps. **(5) WHERE THE INSTINCT IS CONFIRMED RATHER THAN ASSUMED: `attachments`.** All 16 live attachments are dated progress photos — `402006-01_2026-07-16.jpg`, with `uploadedAt` and `uploadedByName` — so `duplicateJob` dropping them is right. That is true of Matrix's data today rather than of the field: an uploaded spec PDF would be indistinguishable, and the rule should be stated as "evidence of work" rather than "files". **(6) A TEMPLATE BUG THAT FALLS OUT OF THE SAME AUDIT:** template save clears `start` and `end` but not `startHour` or `endHour`, which 241 live ops carry — an hour of day with no date.

**BUILT 2026-10-08 — `src/copyRules.js`, THREE LISTS AND THREE NAMED CONSUMERS, AND THE THREE PATHS NO LONGER DECIDE FOR THEMSELVES.** `WORK_RECORD` (what happened), `ENGAGEMENT_IDENTITY` (`jobNumber`, `poNumber`, `dueDate`), `SCHEDULE_FIELDS` (when and by whom), plus `TEMPLATE_KEEPS` and `recordColumnKeys(settings)`. `copyForDuplicate` drops work records AND the engagement; `copyForSplit` drops work records and KEEPS the engagement and `deps`, because the new half is the same work continuing in the same job; `copyForTemplate` drops all three groups except `notes`. `duplicateJob` has no `DROP` list any more, `applySplit` no longer destructures its own four by hand, and `templateOpFromNode`/`nodesFromTemplate` replaced BOTH `loadTemplate`s, which were near-copies that disagreed about feedback. **`notes`: DROP ON DUPLICATE, KEEP ON TEMPLATE, and the reason is the entry's real lesson — THE FIELD IS NAMED FOR ONE THING AND USED FOR ANOTHER, SO THE RULE CANNOT BE DERIVED FROM THE NAME.** A job's notes are a log of what happened to that job (62 of 65 live jobs: `"PB. Status: Crated. Contact Riley. 100%. CFAT."`); a template's notes are a procedure somebody wrote down on purpose. **IF SOMEONE LATER ADDS A REAL DESCRIPTION FIELD, `notes` SHOULD MOVE** and `TEMPLATE_KEEPS` should empty — written into the module header so the next reader finds it there rather than here. **CUSTOM COLUMNS RESOLVE BY TYPE, AND THE FLAG IS LOGGED AS THE CORRECT ANSWER.** `acthours` and `activity` are work records by construction; everything else carries. Type needs no settings surface and works today. **It is a PROXY FOR INTENT and it is already wrong once:** the org's `"Comments"` column is `type: "text"` and holds `"Rittal next Wednesday, 7th"` — a record wearing a descriptive name, which type gets wrong and will keep getting wrong for any column somebody uses as a log. The right answer is a per-column "copies?" flag set by whoever defines the column, because only they know what it is for; that is a settings surface for a problem nobody has hit, so it is logged rather than built, and the suite asserts the known miss as a decision on record rather than leaving it to look like an oversight. **THE DETAIL THAT WOULD HAVE MADE THE WHOLE THING COSMETIC: A CUSTOM COLUMN DOES NOT NECESSARILY STORE UNDER A `_cc_` KEY.** Matrix's `acthours` column writes to `actHours` and its `activity` column to `apprActivity`, both plain field names, resolved as `fieldKey || "_cc_" + id` exactly as the app resolves them — assuming the prefix would have missed both, and `duplicateJob`'s old list dropped `actualHours`, which is not the field that column writes. `settings` is threaded to all four call sites and the suite asserts it ARRIVES, because a shared rule that never sees the org's columns stays green on every other assertion. **RED FIRST, 21 FAILING ASSERTIONS AGAINST THE THREE PATHS AS THEY WERE** — the copy carrying moveLog/placedSubs/notes/PO/dueDate, the split keeping `finishRequests` and clearing `deps`, the template load leaving deps pointing at template ids. `scripts/copy-rules-test.mjs`, 115 assertions, 20 mutants, 20 caught, 0 survived. One survived the first run and found a real gap rather than a bad mutant: nothing asserted what an UNTITLED SUB-OP is named, so the `Op-00n` fallback could have leaked down a level and collided across parents. **AN EXISTING ASSERTION WAS RE-RULED, NOT QUIETLY FLIPPED:** `job-detail-test`'s `"duplicate: keeps client, PM, PO, priority, hpd, notes, due"` asserted the behaviour this entry changes, and is now two checks — what describes the work still comes along, and the engagement and the running log stay behind — with the reason written at the assertion. **WHAT IT DOES NOT CLOSE: the 18 live panels' worth of `placedSubs` and the rest of the residue already in S3 are a separate, finished job (#461's cleanup); this stops new copies MAKING more.** And it does nothing for the copies already made — a job duplicated before today still carries whatever it inherited, and #465 now pins that history from its second save onward. Nothing counts how many such jobs exist. Build green: lint, 105 suites.
